View Details Sponsored Skyrise Apartments, Kampala Luxury Residential Development RFDevelopers is Uganda’s premier developer of high-end residential real estate. Their flagship project is Skyrise Apartments, located in Kololo, Kampala’s most prestigious neighborhood. It features: • 2 and 3-bedroom apartments • Amenities: swimming pool, fully equipped fitness center, club house, BBQ & gazebo terrace, children’s playground, and landscaped gardens Book Service Ad • Skyrise Apartments, Kampala 29s Skip Visit Sponsor A severe public health crisis has erupted on the outskirts of Damaturu, the Yobe State capital, where five residents of the Malam Matari community are currently hospitalized following a vicious attack by a rabid dog. The victims, who sustained deep lacerations and puncture wounds, are undergoing intense medical treatment, including the administration of life-saving post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) vaccines, to halt the progression of the universally fatal zoonotic disease. The Yobe State Government, under the directive of Governor Mai Mala Buni, immediately activated a veterinary rapid response team to contain the outbreak. The incident highlights the chronic vulnerability of semi-urban and rural Nigerian communities to zoonotic pathogens, driven by large populations of unvaccinated stray canines and a systemic lack of public awareness regarding immediate wound management protocols. The Incident in Malam Matari The attack occurred without warning, spreading panic through the densely populated Malam Matari community. The rabid dog engaged in highly aggressive, unprovoked behavior—a hallmark clinical sign of the furious form of rabies encephalitis—before being neutralized by local residents. Laboratory confirmation subsequently verified the presence of the lyssavirus. Veterinary Officer Dr. Yau Buba Wakil, who is leading the state’s sensitization and containment exercise, confirmed that the government has secured and distributed the necessary PEP vaccines. The rapid deployment of the vaccine is critical; once clinical symptoms of rabies—such as hydrophobia, hallucinations, and paralysis—manifest in a human patient, the mortality rate is 100 percent. The Damaturu Local Government, supported by Chief Sanitation Officer Ibrahim Aliyu, has launched a sweeping public health campaign to educate residents on identifying symptomatic animals and the urgent necessity of reporting bites. Global Health Protocols and Kenya Parallels The control of canine rabies is a massive challenge across Sub-Saharan Africa. The World Health Organization (WHO) estimates that rabies kills tens of thousands of people annually across the continent, with children under 15 accounting for 40 percent of fatalities. The global health body mandates that mass dog vaccination campaigns covering at least 70 percent of the canine population are the only proven method to break the transmission cycle to humans. In East Africa, Kenya’s Zoonotic Disease Unit (ZDU)—a collaborative agency between the Ministry of Health and the Ministry of Agriculture—has pioneered a highly effective strategic framework to eliminate human dog-mediated rabies by 2030. The Kenyan model relies on free, decentralized mass vaccination clinics and strictly enforced legislation under the Rabies Control Act, which holds pet owners criminally liable for failing to vaccinate domestic dogs. The integration of such strict legal frameworks presents a viable blueprint for Yobe State and the broader Nigerian federal health architecture. Critical Response Mechanisms Dr. Wakil’s veterinary rapid response team has outlined critical emergency protocols for the residents of Damaturu to prevent further infections. The public health directives include the following vital steps: Immediate Wound Management: Thoroughly washing any dog bite or scratch with soap and running water for a minimum of 15 minutes to physically flush the viral load from the tissue. Urgent Medical Intervention: Seeking immediate clinical care for the administration of the Rabies Immunoglobulin (RIG) and the multi-dose PEP vaccine schedule. Animal Observation: Quarantining the offending animal for 10 days, if safely possible, to observe for clinical signs of rabies. Community Reporting: Utilizing established hotlines to notify the veterinary response team of any stray dogs exhibiting hyper-salivation, unprovoked aggression, or erratic behavior. The Economic Burden of Zoonotic Outbreaks The financial cost of managing a rabies outbreak is staggering. The procurement of human PEP vaccines is notoriously expensive on the global pharmaceutical market, often costing upwards of $50 (KES 6,600) per dose, a price completely out of reach for the average rural Nigerian household. By subsidizing the treatment, the Yobe State government has averted a localized tragedy, but reliance on emergency procurement is fiscally unsustainable. International health experts warn that reactive treatment models must be replaced by proactive vaccination programs. Vaccinating a dog costs mere fractions of a dollar compared to treating a human victim. As urbanization encroaches on wild habitats across Northern Nigeria, the interface between humans and unvaccinated animal populations will only increase. Dr. Wakil urged residents to strictly adhere to the sensitization guidelines, noting that absolute community vigilance is the only permanent defense against the deadly virus. FCT 当局在致命狂犬病爆发后启动紧急疫苗接种活动 - Streamlinefeed.co.ke

FCT Authorities Launch Emergency Vaccination Drive Following Lethal Rabies Outbreak - streamlinefeed.co.ke
正文要点中文精炼2026 年 3 月至 6 月期间,尼日利亚联邦首都直辖区 (FCT) 爆发狂犬病疫情,导致两名居民死亡,另外八人感染,此后,尼日利亚联邦首都特区 (FCT) 启动了针对狗和猫的紧急大规模疫苗接种活动。经国家兽医研究所证实,这些病例发生在瓜瓦拉达 (Gwagwalada) 和夸利 (Kwali) 地区议会。当局将此次疫情的爆发归因于之前疫苗接种工作的系统性失败,导致大量家养动物和流浪动物得不到保护。卫生官员敦促居民限制接种疫苗的宠物,报告动物的攻击性行为,并在被咬伤后立即采取暴露后预防措施。由于担心疫苗供应短缺以及狂犬病症状出现后的高死亡率,该活动旨在覆盖所有六个 FCT 地区议会。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月16日。活动期间:2026年3月至6月(确诊病例)。2026 年 7 月 14 日至 16 日启动紧急应急响应。
地点与对象
尼日利亚联邦首都区 (FCT)。特别是 Gwagwalada 和 Kwali 地区议会。受影响人群:接触过患有狂犬病的流浪狗的居民。疫苗接种目标人群:六个 FCT 地区委员会的所有犬科动物和猫科动物。
事件
FCT 当局和国家兽医研究所 (NVRI) 报告确认狂犬病疫情。两人死亡,八人确诊。在整个 FCT 范围内为宠物发起紧急大规模疫苗接种运动。
规模、影响与风险
8 例人类确诊病例。传播途径:被感染的流浪狗咬伤/抓伤。风险:一旦出现临床症状,几乎 100% 死亡。没有报告卫生系统超负荷或疫苗接种物流以外的运营中断。
应对、状态与不确定性
FCTA 和 NVRI 正在进行调查。为大规模运动采购额外的疫苗剂量。公共建议:限制已接种疫苗的宠物,报告动物行为变化,并在被咬伤后立即寻求暴露后预防 (PEP)。之前的疫苗接种活动(2025年12月至2026年1月)因供应不足而失败。未解决:动物宿主的全部范围和持续的传播链。

Book Service Ad • Novus Consult 28s Skip Visit Sponsor Health and agricultural authorities in Nigeria’s Federal Capital Territory (FCT) have issued an urgent public health alert following a confirmed rabies outbreak that has claimed the lives of two residents. The fatalities, resulting from bites by infected stray dogs, have exposed critical vulnerabilities in the region’s animal vaccination protocols and triggered an immediate crisis response. Dr. Karnak Dandam, Director of Veterinary Services at the FCTA Agriculture and Rural Development Secretariat, confirmed the deaths occurred in the Gwagwalada and Kwali Area Councils. Laboratory analysis conducted by the National Veterinary Research Institute (NVRI) in Vom, Plateau State, verified the presence of the deadly Lyssavirus in eight recent cases across the two districts. The Anatomy of the Outbreak The confirmed cases, recorded between March and June 2026, indicate a sustained circulation of the virus within the local animal population. Of the eight confirmed infections, five originated in Gwagwalada, with the remaining three documented in Kwali. While the FCTA had initiated a vaccination campaign for dogs and cats across the territory in December 2025 and January 2026, Dr. Dandam acknowledged a significant systemic failure: the vaccine supplies were grossly insufficient to cover the target population. Consequently, a substantial reservoir of unvaccinated domestic and stray animals remained vulnerable, facilitating the current transmission chain. Rabies is an acute viral disease that almost exclusively targets the central nervous system. Transmitted via the saliva of an infected animal—typically through a bite or deep scratch—the disease is virtually 100 percent fatal once clinical symptoms emerge. Early human symptoms mirror flu-like conditions, including fever and fatigue, before rapidly progressing to neurological severe distress, hallucinations, and death. Emergency Protocols and Public Advisory In response to the fatalities, the FCTA is aggressively procuring additional vaccine doses to launch a comprehensive mass vaccination campaign targeting every canine and feline across all six area councils. Concurrently, authorities have issued strict directives to residents: Confinement: Pet owners must confine all vaccinated dogs and cats to prevent them from mingling with potentially infected stray populations. Immediate Action: Anyone bitten or scratched by an animal must immediately wash the wound thoroughly with soap and running water—a critical first-aid step that significantly reduces viral load—before seeking emergency post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) at a healthcare facility. Observation: Residents are urged to report any sudden behavioral changes in animals, such as unprovoked aggression, excessive salivation, or unusual isolation. A Broader African Public Health Challenge The lethal outbreak in Abuja highlights a persistent zoonotic threat across the African continent. The World Health Organization (WHO) estimates that rabies causes tens of thousands of deaths annually, predominantly in rural areas of Africa and Asia, with children under 15 being the most frequent victims. The logistical and financial hurdles of maintaining high vaccination coverage in animal populations are mirrored across East Africa. In Kenya, the Zoonotic Disease Unit (ZDU) has implemented a strategic plan aimed at eliminating dog-mediated human rabies by 2030. However, like Nigeria, Kenya struggles with managing expansive stray dog populations and ensuring consistent vaccine cold-chain logistics in remote counties. The cost of a human PEP course—often exceeding tens of thousands of Naira (thousands of KES)—remains prohibitively expensive for low-income families, reinforcing the absolute necessity of subsidizing mass animal vaccinations as the primary preventative measure. Securing the Capital’s Health Infrastructure The tragedy in the FCT underscores the critical importance of the "One Health" approach, which recognizes the deep interconnection between human health, animal health, and the environment. Failing to adequately fund veterinary services directly jeopardizes human lives. As the FCTA scrambles to deploy the newly procured vaccines, the success of the containment strategy will rely heavily on community compliance and the rapid mobilization of veterinary teams to eliminate the viral reservoir in the capital’s outskirts. 布莱恩县蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性 - 爱达荷山快报

Blaine County bat tests positive for rabies - Idaho Mountain Express Newspaper
正文要点中文精炼据中南部公共卫生区报告,爱达荷州布莱恩县的一只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。这篇文章发表于 2026 年 7 月 17 日,日期为 2026 年 7 月 18 日,为该县的居民和游客提供建议。卫生区的报告确认了狂犬病病例,但没有具体说明与该发现相关的规模、影响或风险。证据中没有提供有关应对措施或额外措施的进一步细节。该文章作为公共卫生通知,旨在提高人们的认识并为社区提供建议。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026年7月17日,事件发生于2026年7月18日,证据中未注明时间范围。
地点与对象
该事件发生在美国爱达荷州布莱恩县。受影响的人群包括布莱恩县的居民和游客。
事件
布莱恩县的一只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。中南部公共卫生区报告了这一发现并向公众通报。状态已确认。
规模、影响与风险
证据中没有具体说明规模、影响和风险。没有报告病例数、死亡或暴露情况。
应对、状态与不确定性
中南部公共卫生区正在向居民和游客提供建议。没有提供有关回应的更多细节。证据来自完整的文章正文。

B 常规重要

卫生部长批准国家狂犬病控制框架 - The Nation(巴基斯坦)

Health minister approves national rabies control framework - The Nation (Pakistan )
正文要点中文精炼2026 年 7 月 13 日,巴基斯坦联邦卫生部长批准了国家狂犬病控制框架,并宣布成立一个技术咨询小组来领导预防和应对工作。此举是出于对全国每年估计有 5,000 例狂犬病死亡和数十万例狗咬伤事件的担忧。最近卡拉奇的一起病例凸显了持续的传播风险。该框架旨在加强监测、公众意识和及时的咬伤后疫苗接种,而信德省的 2024 年指南则作为模型提出。目前的差距包括缺乏国家监测系统。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月13日。活动日期:2026年7月13日(会议日期)。
地点与对象
巴基斯坦(全国范围)。弱势群体包括儿童以及农村和服务欠缺城市地区的居民。
事件
联邦国家卫生服务、法规和协调部长批准制定国家狂犬病预防和控制框架,并宣布成立国家技术工作咨询小组,以加强狂犬病预防、监测和应对工作。
规模、影响与风险
巴基斯坦每年估计有 5,000 人死于狂犬病,并发生数十万起狗咬伤案件。最近的病例于 2026 年 7 月 10 日在卡拉奇报告。
应对、状态与不确定性
框架正在开发中。目前尚不存在国家监测系统。强调公众意识运动和疫苗接种。信德省 2024 年指南仅供参考。全国范围内的监督和协调的需求尚未得到解决。

Health minister approves national rabies control framework - The Nation (Pakistan )

nawaiwaqt group News Paper & TV Channel Roznama Nawaiwaqt Waqt News TV Magazines Sunday Magazine Family Magazine Nidai Millat Mahnama Phool Saturday, July 18, 2026 e-Paper Today's Paper EDITOR'S PICKS Newspaper Picks OPINION Editorials Columns Letters NEWS Latest Headlines Top Stories National Sports Lifestyle & Entertainment Business International Editor's Picks Snippets Lahore Karachi Islamabad MULTIMEDIA News In Pictures Cartoons Videos BLOGS Blogs Write for Us AI Use Policy CONNECT WITH US Get Alerts Health minister approves national rabies control framework Web Desk 2:39 PM | July 13, 2026 National Share on Facebook Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on Twitter ISLAMABAD: Federal Minister for National Health Services, Regulations and Coordination Mustafa Kamal on Monday voiced serious concern over the increasing number of rabies cases and dog bite incidents across Pakistan, calling for urgent nationwide measures to tackle the preventable yet deadly disease. The minister made the remarks during a meeting with a delegation from the Indus Hospital & Health Network (IHHN), led by its Chief Executive Officer Dr Abdul Bari Khan, to discuss a comprehensive national strategy for rabies prevention and control. During the meeting, the delegation briefed the minister on the country's rabies situation, highlighting the absence of an effective national rabies surveillance system. BISP payments suspended in Shangla due to biometric SIM shortage According to estimates shared during the briefing, around 5,000 people die from rabies each year in Pakistan, while hundreds of thousands of dog bite cases are reported annually. Children and residents of rural and underserved urban areas are among the most vulnerable. Expressing deep concern over the growing burden of the disease, Mustafa Kamal gave in-principle approval for the development of a National Rabies Prevention and Control Framework aimed at strengthening prevention, surveillance and response efforts across the country. He also announced the formation of a National Technical Working Advisory Group, which will lead the preparation of the framework and coordinate nationwide efforts to combat rabies. Residents demand effective action as stray dog population increases in Rawalpindi The health minister stressed the need for a nationwide public awareness campaign to educate citizens about rabies prevention, the importance of seeking immediate medical treatment after animal bites, and timely vaccination. During the meeting, Dr Abdul Bari Khan presented the minister with the "Sindh Official Guidelines for Prevention of Human Rabies (2024)," developed with the technical assistance of The Indus Hospital and Health Network. The meeting was held days after another rabies case was reported in Karachi. On July 10, a 28-year-old woman, identified as Malookan Bibi from Khwaja Ajmer Nagri in North Karachi, was admitted to the emergency department of Jinnah Postgraduate Medical Centre (JPMC) after developing symptoms of the fatal viral disease. B 常规重要

两只特哈马县蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性;官员敦促宠物接种疫苗并谨慎行事 - KRCR

Two Tehama County bats test positive for rabies; officials urge pet vaccines and caution - KRCR
正文要点中文精炼加利福尼亚州特哈马县的两只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性,促使县卫生官员提醒居民避免接触野生动物并确保宠物接种疫苗。特哈马县卫生服务机构于 2026 年 7 月 15 日确认了这些病例,并强调,如果不及时进行暴露后治疗,狂犬病是致命的。官员们建议彻底清洗伤口,并对任何动物咬伤或抓伤立即就医,同时鼓励居民向当局报告生病或受伤的野生动物。尚未报告人类或家畜病例。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-16。事件日期:报告于2026-07-15(周三)发生。
地点与对象
美国加利福尼亚州特哈马县。公众、宠物和野生动物。
事件

规模、影响与风险
两只确诊狂犬病阳性蝙蝠。没有人类或家畜病例报告。风险被描述为可以通过及时的暴露后治疗和疫苗接种来预防。

(Photo: AdobeStock)Comment on this story2CommentShare storySharetopics: RabiesTehama CountyBatsPet VaccinationsPublic HealthAnimal BitePost-Exposure TreatmentCalifornia Department of Fish and WildlifeRight arrowTEHAMA — Two bats found in residential areas of Tehama County recently tested positive for rabies, prompting county health officials to remind residents to avoid contact with wild animals and ensure pets are vaccinated.The Tehama County Health Services Agency said rabies is a fatal viral disease that affects the central nervous system and can spread to humans and other mammals through the bite of an infected animal. Health officials said the disease is preventable if people take proper precautions and seek treatment promptly after a potential exposure.Officials urged residents not to handle, feed or attempt to capture unfamiliar, stray or wild animals, including bats and feral cats. Pet owners are encouraged to keep animals on a leash during outdoor activities and prevent them from interacting with wildlife.Children also should be taught not to approach unfamiliar animals, even if they appear friendly, according to the agency.Anyone bitten or scratched by an animal should immediately wash the wound thoroughly with soap and water and seek medical attention. Residents who believe they have been exposed to a potentially rabid animal should contact the Tehama County Health Services Agency Public Health Division at (530) 527-6824.Powered By 10 Sec Two Tehama County bats test positive for rabies; officials urge pet vaccines and caution NextStay Health officials also reminded pet owners to keep rabies vaccinations current through a licensed veterinarian.According to the agency, bats are the primary source of human rabies infections in the United States. While domestic animals account for fewer than 10% of reported rabies cases nationwide, rabies remains widespread among bats in Tehama County.Officials cautioned that rabid animals do not always display the commonly associated symptoms of excessive drooling or foaming at the mouth. Instead, infected animals may appear unusually aggressive or tame, stagger, become active during the day if they are normally nocturnal, or show other abnormal behavior.If left untreated, rabies is almost always fatal once symptoms develop. Early symptoms include fever, headache and fatigue before progressing to neurological symptoms such as confusion, hallucinations, difficulty swallowing and paralysis.Health officials said modern post-exposure treatment consists of four vaccine injections administered over two weeks, along with an injection of rabies immune globulin on the first day of treatment. The treatment is highly effective when started promptly after exposure.Residents who observe sick, injured or distressed wildlife are encouraged to report the animal to the California Department of Fish and Wildlife. Sick or injured domestic animals should be reported to the Tehama County Sheriff's Office.--Comment with BubblesJOIN THE CONVERSATION (2)Report a correction or typo. by Taboolaby TaboolaSPONSORED CONTENTCardiologists Say Blood Pressure Isn't Age: Just Stop Drinking This Popular Bedtime Drinktodaybodybalance | SponsoredSponsoredUndoWorrying About Blood Pressure? B 常规重要

罗德岛州的狂犬病病例数量已经超过了近年来的数量。需要知道什么。- 普罗维登斯杂志

Rabies cases in RI are already outpacing recent years. What to know. - The Providence Journal
正文要点中文精炼罗德岛州在 2026 年记录了 25 例动物狂犬病病例,已经超过了五年平均每年 24 例,其中浣熊和蝙蝠是主要携带者。三十二人在潜在暴露后接受了暴露后预防。州卫生官员敦促居民避免接触野生哺乳动物,为宠物接种疫苗,并报告不寻常的动物行为。罗德岛州卫生部和环境管理部强调,如果不及时治疗并协调公共卫生应对措施,狂犬病是致命的。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026-07-16,美国东部时间15:13。事件日期:2026年(截至7月16日)。
地点与对象
美国罗德岛州。受影响的人群包括接触狂犬病的人类和野生哺乳动物(浣熊、蝙蝠、猫、土狼、臭鼬、土拨鼠)。
事件
据州卫生和环境机构证实,罗德岛州狂犬病病例报告称 2026 年将有所增加。官员警告存在致命风险,并建议避免接触野生动物。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年(年初至今,年中)有 25 只动物狂犬病检测呈阳性。三十二人暴露并接受了暴露后治疗。主要携带者:浣熊(14 只)、蝙蝠(7 只),以及猫、郊狼、臭鼬和土拨鼠各一只。风险:如果不及时治疗会致命。通过咬伤、抓伤或唾液接触传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
州官员(RIDOH 和 RIDEM)发布了公共警告和预防指南。RIDOH 提供暴露后评估并根据需要协调疫苗系列。未解决:持续监测动物病例和人类接触情况。2026 年没有人类死于狂犬病的报告。

Rabies cases in RI are already outpacing recent years. What to know. - The Providence Journal

Fetched Original Body
LOCAL Rabies cases in RI are already outpacing recent years. What to know. Thirty-two humans have been exposed to rabies and received treatment in Rhode Island this year Jack Perry Providence Journal July 16, 2026, 3:13 p.m. ET Keep Watching Rhode Island has seen a rise in rabies cases in 2026, surpassing the previous five-year average. State officials warn residents to avoid contact with wild animals to prevent exposure to the fatal disease. Raccoons and bats are the primary carriers of rabies identified in the state this year. An average of 24 animals in Rhode Island tested positive for rabies in each of the last five years, but 25 animals have already tested positive in 2026, with the year little more than half over. Thirty-two humans have been exposed to rabies and received treatment in Rhode Island this year, according to a joint press release from the Rhode Island Department of Health and the Rhode Island Department of Environmental Management. State officials took the opportunity on Thursday, July 16, to again remind Rhode Islanders about the importance of protecting themselves against the disease, which is fatal if not treated in time. Director of Health Dr. Jerry Larkin, said, “Being active in nature regularly is one of the best things you can do for your health. However, you should never touch a wild animal. "Any contact with a wild mammal puts you at risk for rabies.” Where have rabid animals been found? Rhode Island State Veterinarian Dr. Scott Marshall said, "Any direct contact with a wild mammal should be considered a potential rabies exposure." He said people should not approach or feed wild animals and should "report animals behaving unusually to authorities.” The majority of the rabid animals caught this year have been in the northern part of the state, according to state officials. Raccoons have accounted for the most, 14, followed by bats, 7. A rabid cat, coyote, skunk and a woodchuck have also tested positive this year, officials said. Rabies is most commonly transmitted through the bite of an infected animal, but it can also spread when saliva from a rabid animal enters the body through a scratch or an open wound, according to state officials. State law requires all dogs, cats and ferrets to be vaccinated against rabies. State officials say vaccinating pets prevents them from getting rabies and prevents people from becoming exposed to rabies through their pets. The health department recommends the following additional steps to prevent rabies: Avoid all contact with and do not feed stray or free-roaming domestic animals. Avoid all contact with and do not feed wild animals. Do not feed your animals outdoors, as this will attract other animals. This is especially dangerous when feeding large numbers of free-roaming cats. Protect your pets by always maintaining control; walk dogs on a leash or let them play in a fenced yard; and do not let pets wander unsupervised. Securely cover all garbage cans so wild animals cannot scavenge for food. What to do if a person or pet comes into contact with a wild animal Anyone who has had direct contact with a wild mammal should immediately call RIDOH's Center for Acute Infectious Disease Epidemiology at 401-222-2577 (Monday to Friday, 8:30 a.m. to 4:30 p.m. or 401-276-8046 after hours). A RIDOH nurse will assess the exposure and determine whether post-exposure treatment is recommended. If needed, RIDOH will coordinate a post-exposure vaccine series. If any domestic animal, such as pets or livestock, had contact with a wild mammal, or if you are in possession of an animal that you suspect has rabies, call RIDOH, your local animal control officer, and your veterinarian. What a doctor says 'Boo Boo' Andrade eyes comeback after family tragedy Bats sometimes get inside homes, and testing of the bat or preventive vaccination is recommended when a bat has been found in a home where people or pets have been sleeping, or if the bat was around people who do not know if they had contact with the bat. For more information on how to prevent rabies, visit RIDOH’s website. For more information on rabies and ways to protect yourself and your pets, please see this RIDOH fact sheet. 汉考克县蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性 - 广告商论坛报

Hancock County bat tests positive for rabies - Advertiser-Tribune
正文要点中文精炼俄亥俄州汉考克公共卫生局于 2026 年 7 月 15 日报告,在汉考克县一处住宅中发现的一只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。一家人听到噪音后在卧室里发现了这只蝙蝠,这家人把它带到了卫生部门进行检测。俄亥俄州卫生部在 24 小时内确认了阳性结果。卫生官员联系了受影响的家庭,并建议将预防性治疗作为标准方案。这标志着汉考克县自 2009 年以来第一只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。当局敦促谨慎行事,指出狂犬病即使是轻微的咬伤或抓伤也可能传播,一旦出现症状几乎总是致命的。

时间
发布日期:2026年7月16日。事件日期:周三报道(暗示2026-07-15)。时间范围:提供的证据中未报告。

美国俄亥俄州汉考克县。受影响人群:汉考克县的一个家庭在卧室里接触过蝙蝠。
事件

Hancock County bat tests positive for rabies - Advertiser-Tribune

Fetched Original Body
Main Menu Search SiteNewsNewsSportsSportsOpinionOpinionLifeLifeObituaries Obituaries ClassifiedsClassifiedsPublic Notices Public Notices JobsJobsMore Info Main Menu Search SiteNewsNewsSportsSportsOpinionOpinionLifeLifeObituaries Obituaries ClassifiedsClassifiedsPublic Notices Public Notices JobsJobsMore Info SPORTS Email Rejected! x We attempted to send a notification to your email address but we were unable to verify that you provided a valid email address. Please visit my profile to update your email address if you wish to receive notifications. Otherwise, disable notifications and hide this message. Your session was unable to be renewed and will be expiring in 0 seconds. Click here to attempt to renew your session. Daily briefs news Hancock County bat tests positive for rabies By SARA ARTHURS saraarthurs@thecourier.com saraarthurs@thecourier.com FINDLAY — Hancock Public Health reported Wednesday that a local bat has tested positive for rabies. Health Commissioner Lindsay Summit confirmed the bat was found in Hancock County, but declined to specify the location beyond that. “A family woke up to a noise and the bat was in the bedroom,” Summit said. The family brought the bat to the health department, which sent it to the Ohio Department of Health for testing, she said. They received the positive test results within 24 hours. Hancock Public Health then immediately contacted the family and encouraged them to contact their healthcare provider to see if they would need prophylactic treatment, Summit said. She said this is standard protocol. This is the first bat to test positive in Hancock County since 2009, Summit said. “This serves as an important reminder for everyone to take precautions to protect themselves and their families from exposure to bats,” stated the health department in a press release. Bats are the leading cause of rabies deaths for people in the United States. Every year, dozens of bats are submitted and test positive for rabies across the state of Ohio. Unfortunately, you cannot tell if a bat has rabies just by looking at it, and rabies can only be confirmed in a laboratory. If you have been in contact with a bat, even if you are not sure if you have been bitten or scratched, please contact the health department to discuss your rabies exposure risk and whether or not treatment is recommended. If you have any pets that may have been exposed to a bat, please consult your veterinarian for further instructions. The Ohio Department of Health notes that rabies from bats can spread to people even after seemingly minor bites or scratches. Summit added that, in some cases, people may not realize they have been bitten or scratched, so anyone who has had contact with a bat or found a bat in their home should reach out. “Err on the side of caution,” she said. Rabies causes encephalitis, a deadly neurologic disease, and is almost always fatal once symptoms develop, ODH states. Therefore, it is important to see if you might need treatment to prevent symptoms developing. “It is also important to vaccinate your pets against rabies, even if they are only indoor pets,” the ODH website states. “Bats can enter your home through an opening as small as a quarter inch to a half inch and can roost in areas of your home like attics, crawl spaces, and roofing. It is important to examine your home for holes that might allow bats to enter your living quarters.” Whenever possible, the bat should be captured and sent to a laboratory for rabies testing. Hancock Public Health can assist in this process of sending it for testing. “We’re talking about a disease with a 100% mortality rate,” said Dr. Tyler Fields, a veterinarian at Blanchard Valley Veterinary Clinic in Findlay. “It’s not really a disease we want to take a chance on.” Fields and his colleagues regularly hear from owners of indoor cats who decline to get them vaccinated against rabies, assuming they will never be exposed. But even a pet that never leaves the home might still be exposed if another animal gets in, he noted. People do report finding bats in their homes. “Accidents happen,” he said. Also, sometimes indoor cats escape and might roam the neighborhood for a few weeks. Once they come home, it’s hard to know what they might have been exposed to, Fields said. And there is no way to test a living animal for rabies. It can only be diagnosed postmortem, Fields said. Over time, he said, we have gotten better as a society at preventing rabies through vaccination – but this may mean we are further removed from rabies and may not be as aware of why it’s so vitally important to keep up the prevention. He added a professional concern is that the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) recently paused its rabies testing program. News reports from April note that the CDC also paused testing for more than 20 other types of infectious disease. Fields encouraged owners to recognize the importance of prevention by getting their animals vaccinated. And, he said, please call your veterinarian if you have any questions. “We would rather have questions asked than something be assumed,” he said. Summit said that, even without a positive test, Hancock Public Health always assumes that diseases like rabies may be present in the community. She reminded residents to be cautious. For questions and concerns, contact Hancock Public Health at 419-424-7870. Comments (0) Comments are automatically disabled after 48 hours. Rabies-Infected Bat Found At San Diego Zoo Safari Park - Patch

Fetched Original Body
Community Corner Rabies-Infected Bat Found At San Diego Zoo Safari Park The bat is the 19th to test positive for rabies this year in San Diego County. ESCONDIDO, CA — A wild bat found at the San Diego Zoo Safari Park tested positive for rabies, prompting county health officials to ask anyone who may have had contact with the animal to come forward. The deceased bat was found at 12:10 p.m. Saturday at the Oasis on the Rocks restaurant at the Escondido-based park. The animal was collected by a trained Safari Park employee. No human contact with the bat has been reported. The bat was not part of the park's animal collection. It was sent to the San Diego County Public Health Laboratory on Monday, where testing confirmed it had rabies. Find out what's happening in San Diegofor free with the latest updates from Patch. Anyone who touched the bat, or knows someone who may have, was urged to call County Public Health Services at 619-692-8499. Officials said only people who had direct contact with the animal are considered at risk. The bat is the 19th to test positive for rabies this year in the county. In 2025, 21 bats tested positive. Find out what's happening in San Diegofor free with the latest updates from Patch. "Rabies is an extremely serious viral disease, one of the deadliest, with nearly 100% fatality if not caught early enough," said Dr. Ankita Kadakia, the county's deputy public health officer. "We are investigating to ensure anyone who came into contact with the bat receives care quickly. Once symptoms start there is no treatment for rabies." Health officials reminded residents not to touch bats or other wildlife. Anyone who has direct contact with a bat should wash the area with soap and seek medical advice. Rabies symptoms can take weeks or months to appear, but treatment is highly effective when started before symptoms begin. Once symptoms develop, the disease is almost always fatal, officials said. 利物浦女子在希腊度假时被蝙蝠咬伤,接受狂犬病治疗以挽救生命 - Liverpool Echo

Liverpool woman bitten by bat on holiday in Greece given life-saving rabies treatment - Liverpool Echo
正文要点中文精炼2026 年 5 月,一名来自英国利物浦的 53 岁女性在希腊莱斯沃斯岛度假时被蝙蝠咬伤。她没有意识到自己被咬伤,在使用人工智能工具识别该动物后寻求医疗建议,该工具提醒她有狂犬病的风险。她接受了四剂暴露后预防(PEP)课程,前三剂在希腊注射,最后一剂在利物浦注射。由于当地医院的语言障碍,最初的治疗被推迟,但随后的剂量已经完成,没有出现进一步的问题。该事件凸显了接触蝙蝠的风险以及及时就医的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 14 日。活动日期:2026 年 5 月(具体日期未报告)。
地点与对象
英国(利物浦、莫斯利山)。希腊(莱斯沃斯岛、佩特拉、米蒂利尼医院、利物浦热带医学院)。
事件
一名 53 岁的利物浦妇女在希腊度假时被蝙蝠咬伤,随后接受了暴露后狂犬病预防治疗。《利物浦回声报》于 2026 年 7 月 14 日报道了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
一名报告称有被咬伤的情况。没有确诊的狂犬病感染。该妇女接受了狂犬病暴露后预防 (PEP) 疗程,其中包括在希腊注射三剂、在英国注射第四剂。在英国,因接触蝙蝠而传播狂犬病的风险非常严重,但很少见。
应对、状态与不确定性
被咬后的第二天,该妇女前往莱斯博斯岛米蒂利尼医院寻求治疗,并在那里接种了前三剂疫苗。语言障碍最初延迟了治疗,但随后在希腊和英国进行了治疗。最后一剂疫苗于 2026 年 6 月 1 日在利物浦热带医学院注射。由于语言障碍,蝙蝠的狂犬病状况以及初步医疗反应是否充分仍存在不确定性。

Liverpool woman bitten by bat on holiday in Greece given life-saving rabies treatment - Liverpool Echo

Fetched Original Body
Liverpool woman bitten by bat on holiday in Greece given life-saving rabies treatment Laura Horton, from Mossley Hill, didn't realise the dangers when she picked up the bat in her hand Comments 5 News Holly Clarke TV Writer and Katie Pearson 11:37, 14 Jul 2026 Updated 11:37, 14 Jul 2026 View 3 Images The personal trainer from Mossley Hill was on a two-week birthday trip to Lesbos, Greece (Image: Laura Horton / SWNS) A woman from Liverpool was bitten by a bat on holiday in Greece. Laura Horton, 53, from Mossley Hill , requested advice for rabies treatment when she was on her two-week birthday trip to Lesbos in May. Article continues below Powered By 10 10 American recruits a 'couple of divvies' as he learns Scouse slang Share Next Stay The personal trainer shared her experience after she found a bat drowning in the swimming pool at the apartment complex where she was visiting a friend. Laura picked up the animal from the water and let it sit in her hand for 10 minutes to "dry off". She was unaware it had bitten her before flying away. ‌ It was AI tool "Google Lens" which warned Laura of the risks, and suggested she underwent treatment for rabies. ‌ The 53-year-old wanted to know what type of bat she had rescued, so she uploaded a photo of it sitting in the palm of her hand to Google Lens. As well as identifying it as a pipistrelle bat, it alerted her to seek medical treatment immediately due to the risk of contracting rabies from handling the animal. According to the NHS website, rabies is a rare but serious infection that is usually caught from an infected animal's bite or scratch, and it is almost always fatal once symptoms appear. View 3 Images She grabbed the animal from the water and let it sit in her hand for 10 minutes to "dry off" (Image: Laura Horton / SWNS) ‌ She claimed Google Lens warned her that she would be unable to feel a bat bite as their teeth are too small, and on closer inspection, Laura noticed two tiny pin-prick bite marks. Laura went to the Hospital of Mytilene, Lesbos , the following day, on May 8, where she started a course of preventative rabies post-exposure vaccines. She had a total of three jabs in Greece, followed by her fourth and final jab when she returned to the UK in June 1 at the School of Tropical Medicine, Liverpool. Article continues below Laura spoke to Talk to the Press about the experience. She said: "I had no idea you could catch rabies from a bat. I grabbed it from the water on instinct because it was drowning - I had no idea of the risks. "It sat on my hand for about 10 minutes, drying off. We all thought it looked cute, so I took a photo of it." After uploading the image to Google Lens to determine the species of bat, Laura was met with a series of questions from the AI tool. She said: "It started asking questions like, 'Are those your hands?' and 'Have you washed your hands for 15 minutes after handling it?' ‌ "It warned me of the risk of rabies and said I wouldn't feel a bite, as their teeth are too small. I thought it was all a bit dramatic. But when I took a photo of my hand, I saw two tiny bite marks." View 3 Images Laura Horton, 53, was bitten by the bat she rescued from the swimming pool (Image: Laura Horton / SWNS) Laura was four days into her two-week holiday in Petra in Lesbos, with friends when she was bitten. She rang her insurance company which confirmed she should seek medical attention. ‌ She needed to undergo a course of rabies post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) - a life-saving treatment given immediately after a potential bite or scratch from a rabid animal. Later that day, Laura went to the Lesbos hospital but claims a language barrier nearly prevented her from getting the correct treatment. She said: "At first, they tried to send me away with antibiotics. I'm not sure they understood what happened. I was sent to a travel clinic, and a nurse was furious I hadn't been given the first dose yet. I went back to the hospital and found the infectious diseases department - they were brilliant." ‌ She returned on May 11 and May 15 for two more jabs in Greece and had a fourth when she returned to the UK on June 1. She said: "My GP didn't actually know what to do. Luckily, we have the School of Tropical Medicine in Liverpool, and I was able to get my fourth injection arranged with them very easily. "I later found out there is a dedicated rabies hotline in the UK that would have delivered it to my doctor's surgery. It was lucky Google Lens told me to go to hospital - who knows what might have happened. "I read the tragic case about the boy in Canada who died after a bat landed on his face. A lot of people aren't aware of the dangers of these wild animals and the risks. Hopefully I can help raise awareness." Rabies is found throughout the world, but it's very rare in the UK. Rabies is spread by mammals, such as dogs, bats, raccoons and foxes. In the UK, it's only found in some bats. You should get medical attention immediately if: Article continues below you've been bitten or scratched by an animal while you're abroad. an animal has licked your eyes, nose or mouth, or licked a wound you have, while you're abroad. you've been bitten or scratched by a bat in the UK. If you're abroad, get medical help as soon as possible. Do not wait until you get back to the UK

GREENWOOD, S.C. — The South Carolina Department of Public Health has confirmed two animals from different counties have tested positive for rabies. Officials say in Greenwood County, a raccoon was found near Blue Jay and Stevenson Roads that tested positive for rabies. One dog was exposed and is expected to be quarantined in compliance with the South Carolina Rabies Control Act. B 常规重要

狂犬病报告并未引起乔治湖游客的关注 - WRGB

Rabies reports raise little concern among visitors to Lake George - WRGB
正文要点中文精炼据当地卫生部门 2026 年 7 月 13 日的报告,纽约州蒙哥马利县确认富尔顿维尔的一匹马感染浣熊品系狂犬病。蒙哥马利县卫生部门表示,这匹马的检测结果呈阳性,促使兽医采取昂贵的暴露后干预措施。尚未报告与此事件有关的人类病例。虽然公共卫生风险被评估为较低,但该事件凸显了该地区动物狂犬病监测和疫苗接种工作持续保持警惕的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-13。事件日期:所提供证据中未报告。
地点与对象
美国纽约州蒙哥马利县。受影响人群:家畜(马)。
事件
蒙哥马利县富尔顿维尔的一匹马的浣熊品系狂犬病检测呈阳性。蒙哥马利县卫生部门报告为已确认事件。
规模、影响与风险
1 例确诊动物病例(马)。没有人间病例报告。拯救生命的兽用疫苗产生的费用。对公共健康的风险较低,但由于潜在的人类接触而存在。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生部门通过实验室结果确认了浣熊品系狂犬病。所提供的证据中没有报告进一步的调查细节、人体暴露或公共卫生措施。

正文要点中文精炼据渥太华县公共卫生部报告,今年密歇根州渥太华县首例狂犬病确诊病例是在一只蝙蝠身上发现的。没有具体说明发现蝙蝠的具体地点和时间。截至周一,密歇根州各地已发现 21 例蝙蝠感染狂犬病病例,其中一例涉及奥克兰县的一只臭鼬。今年密歇根州没有人感染狂犬病。卫生官员强调为宠物接种狂犬病疫苗的重要性,并建议不要接触野生动物。从五月到九月,接触狂犬病蝙蝠的风险较高。如果怀疑接触,建议立即治疗,并联系渥太华县公共卫生部。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件发生于周三,发布日期为2026-07-16。具体时间范围未明确。
地点与对象
该活动在密歇根州渥太华县举行,特别是在荷兰镇。受影响的人群包括动物,特别是蝙蝠,以及可能接触过病毒的人类。
事件
渥太华县今年首例狂犬病确诊病例是在一只蝙蝠身上发现的。渥太华县公共卫生部报告了狂犬病检测呈阳性的情况,但没有具体说明发现蝙蝠的确切地点或时间。
规模、影响与风险
截至周一,密歇根州各地已发现 21 例蝙蝠感染狂犬病病例,其中一例涉及奥克兰县的一只臭鼬。今年密歇根州没有人感染狂犬病。从五月到九月,接触狂犬病蝙蝠的风险较高。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员建议不要触摸或处理野生动物,并敦促人们为宠物接种狂犬病疫苗。如果怀疑接触,建议立即治疗。对于疑似狂犬病病例,应联系渥太华县公共卫生部。完整的原始文章已被审查。

First Ottawa County rabies case of the year found in bat - AOL.com

Fetched Original Body
HOLLAND TOWNSHIP, Mich. (WOOD) — The first Ottawa County animal case of rabies this year has been discovered after a bat tested positive for the disease Wednesday. The Ottawa County Department of Public Health did not specify where or when the bat was found, saying only it had been informed of the positive rabies test Wednesday. As of Monday, 21 cases of rabies have been found in bats across the state, with only one case of rabies being a skunk found in Oakland County, state data shows . No humans have contracted rabies in Michigan yet this year. The risk of exposure to rapid bats is higher from May through September when bats are most active. Health officials say to never touch or handle wild animals and urge people to vaccinate their pets against rabies. Exposure to rabies happens when people or pets are scratched or bitten by a rabid animal. Animals with rabies do not always appear sick and animals suspected of having rabies should be sent in for lab testing. If you believe you have found an animal that has rabies, contact the Ottawa County Department of Public Health or your local pest control. You can also bring an animal to the Harbor Humane Society for testing if you are able to catch it without making physical contact. If you suspect that you have been exposed to a rabid animal, even while asleep, you should seek treatment immediately, health officials say. Pets who have been exposed to a rabid animal should also receive veterinary care even if they are vaccinated against the disease. Copyright 2026 Nexstar Media, Inc. All rights reserved. 美国爱达荷州布莱恩县。爱达荷州中南部的公众和宠物主人。

规模、影响与风险

应对、状态与不确定性
SCPHD 敦促谨慎行事,对任何潜在接触立即提供医疗建议,并为宠物接种疫苗。提供测试指导。没有提供有关调查状况或所提供证据中报告的其他案件的更多详细信息。

Prev Next Photo by: Scripps National By: KIVI Staff Posted 6:46 PM, Jul 16, 2026 and last updated 7:02 PM, Jul 16, 2026 BLAINE COUNTY — Health officials are urging caution after a bat tested positive for rabies in Blaine County this week.South Central Public Health District officials say this is the first bat to test positive for rabies in South Central Idaho this year, though there have been other cases of bats with rabies in Idaho in 2026.EN ESPAÑOL: Salud confirma caso de murciélago con rabia en el condado de BlaineSCPHD officials are urging people to take precautions around makes and make sure their pets are vaccinated for rabies, as the virus can be deadly to people and pets. 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watchingafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGESCPHD Epidemiology Program Manager Jacob Ward said in a news release that people don't always know they've been exposed to bats and the animals bites can be tough to spot. Ward says, “When there is any possibility of contact with a bat, it's important to seek medical advice right away. Timely treatment is highly effective at preventing rabies after an exposure.”What you should doWhile most bats do not carry rabies, health officials say if you see one make sure to give it plenty of space. They say you shouldn't try to touch, kill, or trap the bat unless you think someone came in contact with the animal and you can do it safely. To protect yourself from rabies they so:Don't touch a bat with your bare hands. Instead wear thick gloves and save the bat in a container.Seek medical attention immediately if there's any potential physical exposure.Vaccinate your pets, including dogs, cats, and horses.Plug all holes in siding and maintain tight-fitting screens on windows. Bats can enter through holes the size of a quarter.If you have do contact with a bat and need it tested, health officials say to call (208) 737-5912 or (208) 737-5971 to speak with a health district epidemiologist. Do not bring live bats into health district offices. 官员称,斯内尔维尔地区被狗袭击的浣熊狂犬病检测呈阳性 - 哥伦比亚广播公司新闻

正文要点中文精炼2026 年 7 月 13 日,佐治亚州格威内特县斯内尔维尔地区的一只浣熊被狗袭击,该县官员于 2026 年 7 月 13 日证实,该浣熊的狂犬病检测结果呈阳性。作为预防措施,参与该事件的三只狗正在接受狂犬病暴露后管理。卫生当局强调,如果没有及时的医疗干预,狂犬病几乎总是致命的,并敦促居民为宠物接种疫苗。未接种疫苗的暴露宠物必须接受四个月的隔离。官员建议对任何咬伤或抓伤立即就医,并鼓励报告动物表现出的异常行为。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布时间:2026年07月13日 19:28 UTC。事件日期:上周(2026年07月06日至2026年07月12日)。
地点与对象
美国佐治亚州格威内特县斯内尔维尔地区。受影响的动物:三只狗和一只浣熊。
事件
格威内特县官员报告说,一只被狗袭击的浣熊狂犬病检测呈阳性。该事件已确认。
规模、影响与风险
一只浣熊狂犬病检测呈阳性。三只狗暴露并接受狂犬病暴露后管理。如果没有症状前的医疗护理,狂犬病几乎总是致命的。佐治亚州是狂犬病流行州。
应对、状态与不确定性
狗正在接受评估并接受狂犬病暴露后管理。接触狂犬病动物的未接种疫苗的狗和猫必须隔离四个月,并在释放前一个月接种疫苗。建议居民确保宠物接种疫苗,如果被咬或抓伤,请立即就医。提供格威内特动物福利和咬伤执法办公室联系方式,用于报告不寻常的动物行为。

CountryNextGen TVSponsoredDigital MarketingGray AI PolicyHeat Advisory is in effectRaccoon attacked by dogs in Snellville area tests positive for rabies Close A raccoon attacked by dogs in the Snellville area last week tested positive for rabies, according to Gwinnett County officials.By Atlanta News First staffPublished: Jul. 13, 2026 at 7:28 PM UTCShareAdd Us On GoogleAdd as a preferred source on GoogleATLANTA, Ga. (Atlanta News First) — A raccoon attacked by dogs in the Snellville area last week tested positive for rabies, according to Gwinnett County officials.Three dogs attacked the raccoon near Tree View Drive on Wednesday. As a precaution, the dogs are under evaluation and undergoing “rabies post-exposure management,” county officials said Monday.Rabies, a virus that attacks the central nervous system, is almost always deadly in humans if they don’t receive medical care before symptoms start. Early symptoms include fever, headache, weakness and discomfort.Residents should make sure their pets are up to date on their vaccines. Gwinnett officials said unvaccinated dogs and cats exposed to a rabid animal must be strictly quarantined for four months and vaccinated one month prior to being released.Anyone who has been bitten or scratched by an animal should seek medical attention immediately.RELATED COVERAGE: Bat tests positive for rabies in DeKalb CountyResidents who see an animal behaving unusually should call the Gwinnett Animal Welfare and Enforcement Bite Office at 770-339-3200, ext. 5576, to have the animal picked up.Georgia is a rabies-endemic state, meaning the virus is present all year long.MORE COVERAGE:Cat tests positive for rabies in DeKalb CountyRaccoon tests positive for rabies in DaculaCoyote tests positive for rabies after attacking Buford manMan tries to rescue raccoon, gets bitten in face while driving, learns it has rabiesRaccoon tests positive for rabies in DeKalb CountyStone Mountain cat tests positive for rabies, DeKalb County officials sayCopyright 2026 WANF. Rabies alert: Raccoon tests positive after Snellville dog attack - FOX 5 Atlanta
正文要点中文精炼2026 年 7 月 8 日,佐治亚州斯内尔维尔,一只浣熊袭击三只狗后确诊患有狂犬病,引发多机构公共卫生警报。格温内特县和 GNR 公共卫生部门向居民发出警告,提醒居民避免行为异常的动物,并指出,如果不及时治疗,狂犬病几乎总是致命的。这三只狗正在接受兽医评估和狂犬病暴露后管理。卫生官员敦促立即就流浪动物或疑似患狂犬病动物的咬伤或抓伤进行医疗救治,并向 GNR 公共卫生部门报告接触情况。这些狗的疫苗接种情况和潜在的更广泛的暴露仍在调查中。

事件
2026 年 7 月 8 日,一只浣熊在斯内尔维尔 Tree View Drive 附近袭击了三只狗后,狂犬病检测呈阳性。格温内特县动物福利和执法部门以及 GNR 公共卫生部门将其报告为已确诊的狂犬病事件。

一只确诊患有狂犬病的浣熊。三只狗被暴露并正在接受兽医评估和狂犬病暴露后管理。风险:如果人类和动物未经治疗,狂犬病几乎总是致命的。所提供的证据中没有报告人类病例。

Public health officials warn Snellville residents to stay away from animals behaving oddly, as the rabies virus is nearly always fatal if untreated. The three dogs involved are undergoing veterinary evaluations and strict rabies post-exposure management. SNELLVILLE, Ga. - A raccoon tested positive for rabies after a dog attack in Snellville, prompting public health officials to issue an immediate warning for residents to avoid animals behaving oddly. Rabid raccoon attack Related SearchesSearch for Budget Senior Monthly Phone Plan on Yahoo. View top results now.Yahoo | SponsoredSponsoredLearn more What we know: A raccoon tested positive for rabies after it was attacked by three dogs on July 8 near Tree View Drive in Snellville. The three dogs involved are currently undergoing veterinary evaluation and rabies post-exposure management, according to Gwinnett County Animal Welfare and Enforcement and GNR Public Health. Officials advise all pet owners to make sure their pets are up to date on their rabies vaccinations. National Association of State Health Veterinarians guidelines state that unvaccinated dogs and cats exposed to a rabid animal must face a strict four-month quarantine and receive a vaccination one month before release. What is rabies? Dig deeper: The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention notes that the rabies virus attacks the central nervous system and is almost always fatal in humans if left untreated. Early symptoms in humans include a fever, headache and general weakness or discomfort. Health officials urge anyone bitten or scratched by a stray or suspected rabid animal to seek immediate medical care and report the exposure. Unusual animal behavior can be reported to the Gwinnett Animal Welfare and Enforcement Bite Office. Public health response What we don't know: Officials have not yet confirmed the current health status of the three dogs or whether they were vaccinated against rabies at the time of the confrontation. It remains unknown if any other wild animals or pets in the Snellville area were exposed to the rabid raccoon before the attack occurred. Protect your family What you can do: If you are bitten or scratched by a stray or suspected rabid animal, seek medical care immediately and tell your healthcare provider about the exposure. After seeking medical treatment, contact GNR Public Health at 770-339-4260 to speak with the on-call epidemiologist. To report an animal acting strangely and request a pickup, call the Gwinnett Animal Welfare and Enforcement Bite Office at 770-339-3200, ext. 5576. The Source: The information in this story was gathered from Gwinnett County Animal Welfare and Enforcement and GNR Public Health, who issued a joint public safety advisory, as well as medical data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention and quarantine guidelines from the National Association of State Health Veterinarians. UPDATE: Two Wicomico Co. Raccoons Test Positive for Rabies in July; 8 Total this Season - WMDT
正文要点中文精炼威科米科县卫生部门报告称,2026 年 7 月,又有两只浣熊狂犬病检测呈阳性,使马里兰州威科米科县的季节性病例总数达到 8 例。最新的病例包括索尔兹伯里 Nithsdale 附近的一例和帕森斯堡 Archie Dennis Road 附近的另一例,其中一例涉及与狗的接触。卫生官员敦促居民避开野生或流浪动物,为宠物接种疫苗,并报告动物咬伤或抓伤情况。如果不治疗,狂犬病是致命的,马里兰州法律规定狗、猫和雪貂必须接种狂犬病疫苗。证据中尚未报告人类病例。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-13。事件日期:2026年7月(两只浣熊在7月检测呈阳性)。时间范围:2026年7月(具体日期在证据中未完全报告)。
地点与对象
美国马里兰州威科米科县。受影响人群:当地居民及家畜(狗)。
事件
据威科米科县卫生部门 (WCHD) 报告,2026 年 7 月,另外两只浣熊狂犬病检测呈阳性。该事件已通过实验室测试得到证实。WCHD 建议居民避免接触野生或流浪动物。
规模、影响与风险
本季度(年初至今)威科米科县浣熊共有 8 例狂犬病阳性病例。2026 年 7 月报告了两起病例:一起发生在索尔兹伯里 Caledonia Drive 的 Nithsdale 附近,另一起发生在帕森斯堡的 Archie Dennis Road 附近,是在与狗接触后发生的。如果不治疗,狂犬病是致命的。证据中没有报告人类病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
WCHD 正在调查并建议居民避免接触野生或流浪动物。预防技巧包括给宠物接种疫苗、限制宠物、教导儿童避开不熟悉的动物、保护垃圾桶以及向 WCHD 报告动物接触(咬伤/抓伤),电话 (410)-546-4446。马里兰州法律要求四个月或以上的狗、猫和雪貂接种狂犬病疫苗。未解决:7 月发生的两起案件的具体日期已部分报告。除了确诊病例之外,没有关于正在进行的接触者追踪或额外测试的信息。

Maryland law requires all dogs, cats, and ferrets aged four months or older to have a current rabies vaccination. If left untreated, rabies is fatal. 乔治湖警员因被死海狸抓伤而被注射狂犬病疫苗 - WNYT.com

Lake George officer gets rabies shot after dead beaver scratch - WNYT.com
正文要点中文精炼2026 年 7 月 10 日,乔治湖公园委员会的一名海上巡逻人员在格伦岛附近被死海狸抓伤后接受了预防性狂犬病疫苗接种。这只海狸后来的狂犬病检测呈阴性,消除了感染的风险。该官员的行为是出于高度谨慎,乔治湖公园委员会于 2026 年 7 月 14 日证实了这一事件。无需采取进一步的公共卫生措施。

时间
发布时间:2026-07-14 下午12:46 ET。事件日期:2026-07-10(周五),报告于2026-07-14发布。
地点与对象
美国纽约州沃伦县乔治湖。海上巡逻官(人类)。
事件
2026 年 7 月 10 日,乔治湖公园委员会的一名海上巡逻人员在被死海狸抓伤后接受了预防性狂犬病疫苗接种。WNYT.com 于 2026 年 7 月 14 日报道了这一事件,并得到了乔治湖公园委员会的证实。
规模、影响与风险
没有确诊的狂犬病病例。这只海狸的狂犬病检测呈阴性。风险被描述为预防性的。
应对、状态与不确定性
官员接受了暴露后预防治疗。海狸尸体狂犬病检测呈阴性。调查结束。没有报告进一步的公共卫生行动。

(WNYT) – A Lake George marine patrol officer got a preventive rabies shot after being scratched by a dead beaver that later tested negative, according to the Lake George Park Commission.The Lake George Park Commission said the scratch happened Friday, July 10, while a marine patrol officer was removing an already deceased beaver from Lake George near Glen Island.“Out of an abundance of caution, the officer received a rabies vaccination as a preventative against potential infection. The recovered animal ultimately tested negative for rabies,” the commission said.(An earlier version of this story wrongly stated the marine patrol officer was attacked by a rabid beaver. B 常规重要

官员警告称,第三巷县蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性 - AOL.com

Third Lane County bat tests positive for rabies, officials warn - AOL.com
正文要点中文精炼俄勒冈州莱恩县于 7 月 13 日报告了 2026 年第三只狂犬病阳性蝙蝠,促使公共卫生官员敦促宠物主人确保狗和猫接种疫苗。今年全州有 13 只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。卫生当局强调,一旦出现症状并通过咬伤或抓伤传播,狂犬病几乎总是致命的。该县建议彻底清洁伤口、进行医疗咨询并向公共卫生部门报告暴露情况,同时不鼓励与蝙蝠直接接触。在俄勒冈州仅检测到与蝙蝠相关的狂犬病病毒株,其他动物也从蝙蝠那里感染了该病毒。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-16。事件报道于 2026 年 7 月 13 日。
地点与对象
美国俄勒冈州莱恩县。普通公众和宠物主人。
事件
莱恩县公共卫生部门报告说,莱恩县的一只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性,这是该县 2026 年第三起此类病例。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年,莱恩县发现了第三只狂犬病呈阳性的蝙蝠。2026 年到目前为止,俄勒冈州全州有 13 只蝙蝠检测呈阳性。狂犬病一旦出现症状,几乎 100% 致命。传播是通过受感染动物的咬伤或抓伤而发生的。
应对、状态与不确定性
官员们敦促对宠物(狗和猫)进行疫苗接种,以在人类和患有狂犬病的野生动物之间建立缓冲。建议公众避免接触蝙蝠,彻底清洁咬伤处,寻求医疗救助,向莱恩县公共卫生部门报告事件,并在可能的情况下捕获蝙蝠进行检测。俄勒冈州仅存在蝙蝠狂犬病病毒株。其他动物从蝙蝠那里感染狂犬病。

Third Lane County bat tests positive for rabies, officials warn - AOL.com

Fetched Original Body
A bat found in Lane County tested positive for rabies , and it's the third one so far this year, Lane County Public Health warned July 13. In the announcement, county and state officials urged members of the public to vaccinate their dogs and cats against rabies if they haven't already. "All pet owners should make certain their dogs and cats are vaccinated against rabies. When our pets are protected from rabies, it provides a buffer zone of immune animals between humans and rabid wild animals, such as bats," said Dr. Emilio DeBess, state public health veterinarian. Thirteen bats have tested positive for rabies in Oregon so far this year. Rabies is a viral disease that affects the nervous system of humans and mammals. It is "almost 100 percent" fatal once symptoms begin, the county said. The virus is carried in the saliva of an infected animal and can be transmitted when that animal bites, or in rarer instances, scratches another animal or person. If you or your pet is bitten by a bat, you should thoroughly clean the wound with soap and water, seek medical attention, report the event to Lane County Public Health and, if possible, capture the bat so it can be tested. There are several strains of rabies found in the U.S. but only bat rabies is found in Oregon. Other animals that have tested positive for rabies in Oregon contracted it from bats. According to Lane County, people should stay away from bats and not handle them. Alan Torres covers local government for the Register-Guard. He can be reached by email at atorres@registerguard.com , on X @alanfryetorres or on Reddit at u/AlfrytRG .
